331390 Let $K$ be a complete graph whose edges are colored red and blue, and let the **$k$-th power of a cycle** be the graph obtained by joining vertices whose cyclic distance is at most $k$. A monochromatic cover consists of vertex-disjoint monochromatic copies of this graph whose vertices cover $V(K)$.

**Grinshpun–Sárközy powers-of-cycles conjecture.** The vertex set of every $2$-colored complete graph can be covered by at most

$$
ck
$$

vertex-disjoint monochromatic $k$-th powers of a cycle, for an absolute constant $c$.

The known bound is exponential in $k\log k$; the conjectured linear dependence on $k$ remains open.
331409 Let $(B_n)$ be the sequence of Chocolate Numbers, and let $p$ be a prime. Consider the sequence modulo $p$. **Zero-congruence conjecture.** The sequence $B_n$ considered modulo prime $p$ converges to the all-zero sequence if and only if $p=2$, $p=5$, or $p\equiv \pm1\pmod{5}$. This conjecture is motivated by the corresponding proved characterization for the auxiliary product sequence $P_n$ and by computational experiments; its general validity for $B_n$ remains open.
331410 Let $(B_n)$ be the sequence of Chocolate Numbers, and let $p$ be a prime such that $p\ne2$, $p\ne5$, and $p\not\equiv\pm1\pmod{5}$. **Period-divisibility conjecture.** The sequence $B_n$ modulo $p$ repeats with a period divisible by $p(p-1)$. The conjecture is based on computational experiments and on the proved periodicity behavior of the auxiliary sequence $P_n$, assuming a connection between the behaviors of $B_n$ and $P_n$; it remains open.
331433 Let $n$ be a positive integer. A **rank-symmetric chain** in the Boolean lattice $2^{[n]}$ is a chain whose numbers of elements in complementary ranks are equal.

**Rank-symmetric chain conjecture.** The Boolean lattice $2^{[n]}$ can be partitioned into $\binom{n}{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}$ rank-symmetric chains such that the size of any two chains differ by at most $2$.

This is proposed as a rank-symmetric variant of Füredi's conjecture. The source gives no resolution, so the conjecture remains open.
331439 Let $E\subset\mathbb{N}$ be additively and multiplicatively piecewise syndetic, and suppose that $E$ has positive lower density. **The piecewise syndetic $\{x+y,xy\}$ conjecture.** The set $E$ contains many configurations of the form

$$
\{x+y,xy\}.
$$

The conjecture is motivated by the dichotomy for two-cell partitions: one cell either has upper density $1$ or both cells have positive lower density. Its relation to $DC$ sets and the instability of positive lower density under partitions leave the assertion open.
331445 Let $P$ be a polytope. In McMullen's formula, write the local contribution associated with a face $F$ as $\alpha(F,P)$, and use the Berline–Vergne construction for these local terms, called the **BV-$\alpha$-valuation**. The polytope $P$ is **BV-$\alpha$-positive** when all BV-$\alpha$ values associated with $P$ are positive.

**BV-alpha positivity conjecture.** Every regular permutohedron is BV-$\alpha$-positive.

The conjecture is based on computations in small dimensions, where the symmetry of regular permutohedra makes the recursive BV-$\alpha$-valuation tractable. A general proof is not supplied and the conjecture remains open.
331459 Let $G_1$ and $G_2$ be connected graphs, and let ${\mathcal B}_{G_1}^T$ and ${\mathcal B}_{G_2}^T$ denote their associated algebras. For a graph, its **bridge-free matroid** is the graphical matroid of the graph obtained by removing all bridges.

**Converse conjecture.** The algebras ${\mathcal B}_{G_1}^T$ and ${\mathcal B}_{G_2}^T$ are isomorphic if and only if the bridge-free matroids of $G_1$ and $G_2$ are isomorphic.

The forward implication is conjectured to provide a converse to the preceding result, which establishes that isomorphic bridge-free matroids imply isomorphic algebras. The source gives no resolution of the converse implication.
331490 Let $G$ be a graph with no bridges and no loops. Let $\tau(G)$ be its number of spanning trees, let $(G)$ be its number of acyclic orientations, and let $^*(G)$ be its number of totally cyclic orientations. **Graphic Merino–Welsh conjectures.** The following inequalities hold:

$$
\max\left((G),^*(G)\right)\geq\tau(G),
$$

$$
(G)+^*(G)\geq 2\cdot\tau(G),
$$

and

$$
(G)\cdot^*(G)\geq\tau(G)^2.
$$

The third, multiplicative, inequality is the strongest and implies the additive inequality, which in turn implies the first. The conjectures have been proved for several graph families, including wheels, complete graphs, and series-parallel graphs, but remain open in general.
331499 Let $\mathcal{V}$ be a positive vertical path with $u$ up-steps and $d$ down-steps, let $Q$ be the set of quarter-planar paths with $r$ right-steps, $l$ left-steps, and vertical projection $\mathcal{V}$, and let $G_1$ and $G_2$ be respectively the peak-count and signed peak-count generating functions over $Q$. A polynomial is **$(x+1)$-positive** when it belongs to $\mathbb{N}_0[x+1]$. **The equivalence conjecture.** The polynomial $G_1$ is $(x+1)$-positive if and only if $G_2$ is $(x+1)$-positive. The conjecture was tested for $u,d,r,l\leq 5$ and is proposed because the signed generating function has an explicit parameter dependence; proving it would imply the preceding positivity conjecture.
331500 Let $\mathcal{V}$ be a positive vertical path with $u$ up-steps and $d$ down-steps, let $Q$ be the set of quarter-planar paths with $r$ right-steps, $l$ left-steps, and vertical projection $\mathcal{V}$, and let $G_1$ be the peak-count generating function over $Q$. A polynomial is **$(x+1)$-positive** when it belongs to $\mathbb{N}_0[x+1]$. **The parameter-dependence conjecture.** Whether $G_1$ is $(x+1)$-positive depends only on $r$, $l$, $u$, and $d$. The claim is presented as a consequence of the equivalence conjecture together with the theorem that $G_2$ depends only on these four step counts; the paper notes that $G_1$ itself need not depend only on them.
331552 Let $G$ range over finite nonabelian simple groups, and call $G$ **cubic-GRR-free** if it has no cubic graphical regular representation. **Finiteness conjecture.** There are only finitely many finite nonabelian simple groups that are cubic-GRR-free. This is posed as a problem motivated by the classification of cubic GRRs for $\mathrm{PSL}_2(q)$; the source gives no resolution of the finiteness assertion.
331743 Let $(G,v)$ be a rooted graph. For an almost complete rotation system at $v$, let the **local genus polynomial** be the genus polynomial of the embeddings consistent with that system. **Local log-concavity conjecture.** All the local genus polynomials of every rooted graph $(G,v)$ are log-concave. This is the local form of the conjecture that genus distributions are log-concave; its equivalence with the CLLC formulation is stated in the source, while the general claim is presented as unresolved here.
331750 Let $G$ be a permutation group. Its **real closure** is the closure defined using the real permutation module, and its **strong $2$-closure** is the group preserving the relevant strong orbital structure. **Real-closure conjecture.** The real closure of $G$ coincides with its strong $2$-closure.

The claim is presented as a conjecture in the source, but the supplied material gives no resolution evidence. It is therefore recorded as open.
331768 Let $G$ be a connected directed graph. An **antimagic labeling** is an edge labeling in which each label is a distinct element of $\{1,2,\dots,|E|\}$ and the oriented sums at the vertices are pairwise distinct, where an oriented sum is the sum of labels on edges directed away from the vertex minus the sum of labels on edges directed towards it. **Directed Antimagic Graph Conjecture.** Every connected directed graph except for the directed graphs $K_3$ and $K_{1,2}$ admits an antimagic labeling. This directed analogue was suggested by Hefetz, Mütze, and Schwartz; the stated exceptions are the cyclic orientation of $K_3$ and the specified orientation of $K_{1,2}$.
331795 Let $H$ be an irreducible affine hypersurface in $\mathbb{A}^s\times\mathbb{A}^s$. It is **$(s,t)$-grid-free** when the associated bipartite algebraic graph contains no complete bipartite subgraph with $s$ vertices in the first part and $t$ vertices in the second. If $H$ is $(s,t)$-grid-free, then there exist $\sigma\in\operatorname{Aut}(\mathbb{A}^s)$ and $F(\overline{x},\overline{y})\in\mathbb{C}[\overline{x},\overline{y}]$ of degree at most $d$ in $\overline{y}$, for some $d=d(s,t)$, such that

$$
H=\{F\circ(\operatorname{id}\times\sigma)=0\}.
$$
 This is the affine special case of the proposed bounded-degree equivalence principle, with polynomial automorphisms replacing Cremona transformations; the paper presents it as a conjecture and does not resolve it in general.
331833 Let $n\geqslant 3$ and let $G$ be a connected graph on $n$ nodes with at most

$$
\left\lfloor\frac{\binom{n}{2}}{2}\right\rfloor
$$

edges. Write $\mathcal{P}_{n,G}$ for the corresponding graph invariant, and let $H$ range over graphs on at most $n-1$ nodes. **Polynomial dependence conjecture.** $\mathcal{P}_{n,G}$ is a polynomial in some invariants $\mathcal{P}_{n,H}$ with $H$ ranging over graphs on at most $n-1$ nodes. This proposes a reduction of the invariants associated with sufficiently sparse connected graphs to invariants arising from graphs with fewer nodes; the supplied text gives no resolution status.
331841 Let $G$ be a connected graph of order $n$, and let $b(G)$ denote its burning number. **Bonato, Janssen, and Roshanbin's conjecture.**

$$
b(G)\leq \left\lceil\sqrt{n}\right\rceil.
$$

This conjecture would improve the general bound $b(G)\leq 2\left\lceil\sqrt{n}\right\rceil-1$ and seeks the sharp order-of-magnitude upper bound for the burning number of connected graphs. It is presented as an open problem.
331969 Let $G$ be a large but finite graph with positive curvature. Let the **demand** and **inertia** of a vertex be the graph quantities defined in the paper. **Jonckheere–Lou–Bonahon–Baryshnikov's balance conjecture.** The graph has more balanced values for demand and inertia than a graph with negative curvature. The surrounding text gives positive-curvature counterexamples with highly unbalanced demand and inertia, so the formulation is not supported by those examples.
331981 Let $P$ and $Q$ be finite posets, and let $J_{P,Q}$ be the defining toric ideal of the isotonian algebra $K[P,Q]$. A **poset cycle** of length $\ell$ is a subposet with vertices $a_1,\ldots,a_\ell,b_1,\ldots,b_\ell$ and covering relations

$$
a_i<b_i,\qquad a_i<b_{i+1}\quad (1\leq i\leq \ell),
$$

where $b_{\ell+1}=b_1$. An induced poset cycle is one induced by the relevant vertices.

**Quadratic-binomial generation conjecture.** If $Q$ does not contain any induced poset cycle of length greater than $4$, then $J_{P,Q}$ is generated by quadratic binomials.

This is the sufficient-condition direction of the paper’s broader quadratic-generation conjecture and is supported by the stated Gröbner-basis results and experiments. Its general validity remains open.
332002 Let $D_n$ be a dihedral group with an order $n$ cyclic subgroup, and let $S$ be a generating set composed of three involutions, none of which belongs to that cyclic subgroup. Write $\lambda_1(D_n,S)$ for the first length parameter associated with $(D_n,S)$. **Three-involution bound.** We conjecture that

$$
\lambda_1(D_n,S) \leq \left\lfloor \frac{n}{2} \right\rfloor + 1.
$$

A complete proof is described as currently elusive; the theorem presented afterward supplies strong evidence for the bound in particular three-involution generating sets.

  "331097": {
    "mathdb_id": 331097,
    "title": "Compressed zero-divisor graph description for ideals in UFDs",
